diff --git a/pm/cycle1/screenshots/tools/size-split-states.cjs b/pm/cycle1/screenshots/tools/size-split-states.cjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..c5c1bfa
--- /dev/null
+++ b/pm/cycle1/screenshots/tools/size-split-states.cjs
@@ -0,0 +1,150 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env node
+// Captures the Size-Split Delta table states the UX notes list
+// (pm/cycle2/ux-notes-size-split-delta.md, section 3) from a running web app
+// fed by the fixture server in TAPE=size-split mode. The table changes with
+// the live minute, so the script polls the three table rows and saves a
+// zoomed-out and a zoomed-in screenshot the first time each state appears,
+// for up to MINUTES minutes. Optional DARK=1 switches the chart to the dark
+// theme first; DIALOGS=1 also shoots the settings dialog (Inputs with Advanced
+// closed and open, both with Fixed size selected, and Visibility);
+// CONFLICT=1 switches the conflict circles on before polling.
+//
+// Usage: BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8010/ MINUTES=6 node size-split-states.cjs <outDir> <prefix>
+const path = require("node:path");
+const fs = require("node:fs");
+const { chromium } = require("playwright");
+
+const outDir = process.argv[2];
+const prefix = process.argv[3] ?? "state";
+if (!outDir) { console.error("outDir required"); process.exit(2); }
+fs.mkdirSync(outDir, { recursive: true });
+const LABEL = "Size-Split Delta (large vs small)";
+const minutes = Number(process.env.MINUTES ?? 6);
+const dark = process.env.DARK === "1";
+const dialogs = process.env.DIALOGS === "1";
+const conflict = process.env.CONFLICT === "1";
+const slug = (s) => s.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, "-").replace(/(^-|-$)/g, "");
+
+/** The state a table reading belongs to, by the UX notes' list; null when it is not one we collect. */
+function stateOf(rows) {
+  const [a, b, c] = rows;
+  if (c.startsWith("Needs live tape · no feed")) return "05-rest-no-feed";
+  if (c.startsWith("Tape live · next bar")) return "01-tape-live-next-bar";
+  if (c.startsWith("Learning sizes")) return "02-learning-sizes";
+  if (a.startsWith("Need ")) return "03-need-valid-bars";
+  if (c.startsWith("Tape ") && c.includes("below")) return "04-tape-share-below";
+  if (a.includes("confirms")) return "07-large-confirms";
+  if (a.includes("lags")) return "08-large-lags";
+  if (a.includes("no extreme") || a === "No extreme") return "06-forming-no-extreme";
+  return null;
+}
+
+async function tableRows(page) {
+  return page.evaluate(() => {
+    const cells = [...document.querySelectorAll(".draw-table-overlay td, .draw-table-overlay .draw-table-cell, .draw-table-overlay div")];
+    const texts = cells.map((cell) => cell.textContent?.trim() ?? "").filter((text) => text.length > 0);
+    return texts.slice(0, 3);
+  });
+}
+
+async function addIndicator(page) {
+  await page.getByRole("button", { name: /Indicators/ }).click();
+  await page.getByLabel("Search indicators").fill(LABEL);
+  const row = page.locator(".indicators-row").filter({ hasText: LABEL }).first();
+  await row.waitFor();
+  if ((await row.getAttribute("aria-pressed")) !== "true") await row.click();
+  await page.getByRole("button", { name: "Close indicators" }).click();
+}
+
+async function openSettings(page) {
+  const legendRow = page.locator(".indicator-legend-row").filter({ hasText: LABEL }).first();
+  await legendRow.hover();
+  await legendRow.getByRole("button", { name: "Indicator settings" }).click();
+  await page.waitForSelector(".indicator-settings-modal");
+  await page.waitForTimeout(300);
+}
+
+async function closeSettings(page) {
+  const ok = page.locator(".indicator-settings-modal").getByRole("button", { name: /^Ok$/ });
+  if (await ok.count()) await ok.click();
+  else await page.keyboard.press("Escape");
+  await page.waitForTimeout(300);
+}
+
+(async () => {
+  const browser = await chromium.launch();
+  const page = await browser.newPage({ viewport: { width: 1600, height: 1000 }, deviceScaleFactor: 1 });
+  page.on("pageerror", (e) => console.log("pageerror:", e.message));
+  await page.goto(process.env.BASE_URL ?? "http://127.0.0.1:8000/", { waitUntil: "domcontentloaded" });
+  await page.waitForSelector(".chart-mount canvas", { timeout: 60_000 });
+  await page.waitForFunction(() => document.body.innerText.includes("LIVE"), null, { timeout: 60_000 });
+  if (dark) await page.evaluate(() => document.documentElement.setAttribute("data-theme", "dark"));
+  await addIndicator(page);
+  await page.waitForTimeout(1500);
+  if (conflict) {
+    await openSettings(page);
+    await page.getByRole("tab", { name: "Visibility" }).click();
+    await page.getByLabel("Show conflict circles").check();
+    await closeSettings(page);
+    await page.waitForTimeout(1500);
+  }
+  const chart = page.locator(".chart-mount").first();
+  const box = await chart.boundingBox();
+  const cx = box.x + box.width * 0.6;
+  const cy = box.y + box.height * 0.4;
+  // Double-clicking the time axis resets the time scale (axisDoubleClickReset), so every
+  // state is shot from the same default view instead of the previous state's residual zoom.
+  const resetZoom = async () => {
+    await page.mouse.dblclick(box.x + box.width * 0.5, box.y + box.height - 12);
+    await page.mouse.move(box.x + 5, box.y + 5);
+    await page.waitForTimeout(500);
+  };
+  const zoom = async (direction, steps) => {
+    await page.mouse.move(cx, cy);
+    for (let i = 0; i < steps; i += 1) await page.mouse.wheel(0, direction * 400);
+    await page.mouse.move(box.x + 5, box.y + 5);
+    await page.waitForTimeout(700);
+  };
+
+  if (dialogs) {
+    await openSettings(page);
+    const tierMode = page.locator(".indicator-settings-modal select").first();
+    await tierMode.selectOption({ label: "Fixed size" });
+    await page.waitForTimeout(400);
+    await page.screenshot({ path: path.join(outDir, `${prefix}-dialog-inputs-advanced-closed.png`) });
+    const advanced = page.locator(".indicator-settings-modal").getByText(/^Advanced/).first();
+    if (await advanced.count()) { await advanced.click(); await page.waitForTimeout(400); }
+    await page.screenshot({ path: path.join(outDir, `${prefix}-dialog-inputs-advanced-open.png`) });
+    await page.getByRole("tab", { name: "Visibility" }).click();
+    await page.waitForTimeout(400);
+    await page.screenshot({ path: path.join(outDir, `${prefix}-dialog-visibility.png`) });
+    await page.getByRole("tab", { name: "Inputs" }).click();
+    await tierMode.selectOption({ label: "Largest 2% of prints" });
+    await closeSettings(page);
+    await page.waitForTimeout(1500);
+  }
+
+  const seen = new Set();
+  const deadline = Date.now() + minutes * 60_000;
+  let last = "";
+  while (Date.now() < deadline) {
+    const rows = await tableRows(page);
+    const key = rows.join(" | ");
+    if (key !== last) { console.log(new Date().toISOString().slice(11, 19), key); last = key; }
+    const state = rows.length === 3 ? stateOf(rows) : null;
+    const divergence = rows[1]?.startsWith("Divergence") ? "09-divergence-row" : rows[1]?.startsWith("Conflict") ? "10-conflict-row" : null;
+    for (const name of [state, divergence].filter(Boolean)) {
+      if (seen.has(name)) continue;
+      seen.add(name);
+      await resetZoom();
+      await zoom(1, 10);
+      await page.screenshot({ path: path.join(outDir, `${prefix}-${name}-zoomed-out.png`) });
+      await zoom(-1, 22);
+      await page.screenshot({ path: path.join(outDir, `${prefix}-${name}-zoomed-in.png`) });
+      console.log("saved", name, "rows:", key);
+    }
+    await page.waitForTimeout(4000);
+  }
+  console.log("states captured:", [...seen].sort().join(", ") || "none");
+  await browser.close();
+})().catch((error) => { console.error(error); process.exit(1); });

# Worker brief: building an indicator for Terminal-L1-L2

You are one of several worker agents. A project manager (PM) agent assigned you
exactly one indicator, named in your task prompt. Build it completely: code,
settings schema, tests, registry entry, Historical Mode policy, and a short doc
page. Commit on your own branch in your own worktree. Do not push. Do not ask
the PM questions; make a sensible decision, write it down in your report.

## Repository and environment

Repository root (the PM's checkout): `/home/user/Terminal-L1-L2/Terminal-L1-L2-development-indicator-sdk`
You work in a separate git worktree of that repository; your task prompt or your
shell's working directory tells you where. Everything below is relative to your
worktree root.

The project pins Node 24.21.0. Before running anything:

```bash
export PATH=/root/.nvm/versions/node/v24.21.0/bin:$PATH
node --version   # must print v24.21.0
```

Dependencies are installed only in the PM's checkout. Link them into your
worktree once (they are gitignored, so the links never enter a commit):

```bash
ROOT=/home/user/Terminal-L1-L2/Terminal-L1-L2-development-indicator-sdk
ln -s $ROOT/node_modules ./node_modules
ln -s $ROOT/apps/web/node_modules ./apps/web/node_modules
```

Commands you will use (run from the worktree root unless noted):

| Purpose | Command |
|---|---|
| Your indicator's tests only | `cd apps/web && npx vitest run src/lib/indicators/<folder>` |
| Tests that enumerate the registry | `cd apps/web && npx vitest run src/lib/chart src/components` |
| Whole web suite (do this once before you finish) | `cd apps/web && npx vitest run` |
| Type check | `cd apps/web && npx tsc -b` |
| Web lint | `cd apps/web && npx oxlint` |
| Repo lint | `npx eslint .` |
| Docs check | `node scripts/validate-docs.cjs` |

All of these must pass before you commit. The runtime (jest) suite and the
Python conversion suite are unaffected by indicator work; skip them.

## Read these first, in this order

1. `docs/architecture/indicators/data-for-indicator-authors.md` (the rules every indicator follows)
2. `apps/web/src/lib/chart/indicatorTypes.ts` (the authoring contract: `RuntimeIndicatorDef`, `IndicatorBarCtx`, `SettingsFieldSchema`, `PlacementSpec`)
3. `apps/web/src/lib/chart/indicatorRegistry.ts` (where you register, and `HISTORICAL_INDICATOR_POLICIES`)
4. `docs/architecture/indicator-sdk/TA.01-indicator-authoring-core-evidence.md` (execution model: incremental `onBar`, `replace`, replay, fault latching)
5. `docs/architecture/indicator-sdk/TA.02-ta-math-library-evidence.md` and `apps/web/src/lib/chart/ta/index.ts` (the `ta.*` factories: create them once in `createState`, never in `onBar`)
6. `docs/architecture/indicator-sdk/TA.03-plotting-rendering-wrapper-evidence.md` (`ctx.draw.fill`, `ctx.markers.plotshape/plotchar`, `draw.line/box/label/table` handles)
7. `docs/architecture/indicators/setting-presets.md` (every setting key must be in the schema, `step > 0` for numeric fields)
8. Reference indicators: `apps/web/src/lib/indicators/rsi/rsi.ts` and its tests (simplest SDK indicator); `apps/web/src/lib/indicators/large-trade-bubbles/largeTradeBubbles.ts` (markers plus `tradeAvailability` gating); `apps/web/src/lib/indicators/vwap-bands/vwapBands.ts` and its tests (reading the tick store through `ctx.ticks`, and how tests fake it); `apps/web/src/lib/indicators/l2-feed-status/l2FeedStatus.ts` (reading `ctx.l2`)
9. The test harness `apps/web/src/lib/chart/replayBars.ts`

## What you deliver

1. **One folder** `apps/web/src/lib/indicators/<folder>/` owning the whole
   indicator: pure computation in its own module, the `RuntimeIndicatorDef` in
   `<name>Indicator.ts` (or `<name>.ts` for a simple one), and `tests/`.
2. **Registration**: add the import, append the def to the end of
   `INDICATOR_REGISTRY`, and append an entry to the end of
   `HISTORICAL_INDICATOR_POLICIES`, both in `apps/web/src/lib/chart/indicatorRegistry.ts`.
   Append, never insert in the middle: several workers edit this file and the
   PM merges the branches.
3. **Historical Mode policy** that is honest: `OHLCV_POLICY` for candle-only
   math, `CANDLE_FLOW_POLICY` when you read buy/sell sides, and an
   `unavailableReason` sentence for anything that needs live ticks or the live
   order book. Never fabricate a value a candle archive does not contain.
4. **Settings schema** (`settingsSchema`) covering every key in
   `defaultSettings`, with sensible `min`/`max`/`step`, `affects` set correctly
   (`recompute` for anything that changes the math, `cosmetic` for colors), and a
   `staticHint` explaining the data the indicator needs when it is live-only.
   `apps/web/src/components/tests/indicatorPresetInputs.test.ts` fails if a
   settings key is missing from the schema.
5. **Tests** in `tests/`: exact hand-computed values for the math, `replace:
   true` semantics (a revised forming bar does not corrupt committed state),
   empty input, insufficient warm-up giving `null` (never `NaN`), and the
   data-availability gates (an unavailable source yields `null`, not zero).
   Use `replayBars` for bar sequences and a hand-built ctx for `replace` cases,
   as `rsi/tests/rsi.test.ts` does.
6. **A doc page** `docs/architecture/indicators/<name>.md`: what it computes,
   the data family it reads, units, what happens when the data is missing,
   settings and defaults, and how a retail trader reads it. Add one row to the
   "Documentation map" table in `docs/architecture/indicators/README.md`. Keep
   links relative and valid; `node scripts/validate-docs.cjs` checks them.
7. **One commit** on your branch, message in the imperative, describing what the
   indicator does. Use `git -c user.name="Worker" -c user.email="worker@chartprime.local" commit`.
   No model names anywhere in the commit or code.

## Rules that are not negotiable

- Read buy/sell through `barSides` (`apps/web/src/lib/indicators/barSides.ts`).
  Never compute sell as `volume - buy_volume`. A bar `barSides` reports as not
  `known` draws no delta (`null`), not zero.
- `missing` is not `empty`. Unavailable data yields `null`; a quiet bar with
  complete data yields the real value (which may be 0).
- Tick-based indicators read `ctx.ticks` (declare `ticksWanted: true`) and must
  check `ticks.coverageOf(bar.ts_open, bar.ts_open + bar.interval_ms)`:
  `"all"` is complete, `"thin"` means only large prints are present, `"none"`
  means nothing. Anything that counts or sizes trades is valid only on `"all"`.
  `ctx.trades` with `tradeAvailability === "LIVE_COMPLETE"` is the per-bar
  alternative when you only need the forming bar's prints.
- Order-book indicators read `ctx.l2`. Output `null` unless
  `l2.availability === "LIVE"`, `l2.trusted()` is true and the channel you read
  reports `synced`. Check `apps/web/src/lib/chart/indicatorL2.ts` and
  `apps/web/src/lib/l2/types.ts`.
- Quantities stay in provider units (Binance base asset, futures contracts,
  equity shares). Label them; never convert to a notional the provider did not
  report, except the documented Binance `price * size` USD notional.
- Gate on capabilities and availability, never on `provider === "..."`, except
  to label units (as `largeTradeBubbles.ts` does).
- Incremental only: `onBar` nudges state forward one bar; never rebuild history
  from an array. Respect `replace` (a forming bar revised in place). `ta.*` and
  `draw.*` factories are created once in `createState`.
- Fail closed to `null`. Never emit `NaN` or `Infinity`; the runtime latches
  the indicator as faulted.
- Match the surrounding code style (2-space indentation, double quotes,
  trailing commas, `readonly` where the codebase uses it). Do not reformat lines
  you did not change. No new dependencies.
- Touch only: your folder, `indicatorRegistry.ts` (append only),
  `docs/architecture/indicators/README.md` (one table row), your doc page, and
  `indicatorPresetInputs.test.ts` only if you must list a key in
  `NOT_IN_SDK_PRESETS` (prefer not to).

## Quality bar

The owner's goal is "top grade, very useful and marketable indicators for
retail traders." That means: defaults a trader can use without reading docs,
a label a trader recognizes, colors that read at a glance (bullish green
`#26a69a` / bearish red `#ef5350` are the house pair; neutral `#d1d4dc`),
honest nulls during warm-up, and a settings dialog that exposes what traders
actually tune. Prefer one excellent indicator over two mediocre ones.

## Your report back to the PM

End with a short report containing: branch name, worktree path, files
added/changed, the exact test and lint commands you ran with their pass/fail
counts, every design decision you made that the brief left open, and anything
you could not verify (for example, live rendering, which no worker can see).
# Platform capability sheet (maintained by the PM)

What an indicator in this terminal can read, draw, and configure. Specialists
design against this sheet, not against what they wish existed. Code wins over
this sheet; report a disagreement to the PM instead of working around it.

Snapshot: `Terminal-L1-L2-development-indicator-sdk`, imported 2026-10-26.
Authoritative files: `apps/web/src/lib/chart/indicatorTypes.ts` (contract),
`indicatorRegistry.ts` (registration and Historical policies),
`indicatorTicks.ts`, `indicatorL2.ts`, `indicatorLiquidity.ts`,
`apps/web/src/types/frames.ts` (wire shapes),
`docs/architecture/indicators/data-for-indicator-authors.md`,
`docs/architecture/l2/l2-sdk.md`.

## 1. Instruments and feeds

| Instrument | Live source | What arrives live | Historical Mode |
|---|---|---|---|
| BTC, ETH spot and perpetual | Binance WebSocket | every trade with taker side; aggregated depth diffs per price (no individual orders); 1m bars | 1m/1h/1d candles with taker buy volume; verified L2 book states for some August 2026 hours only |
| ES, NQ (continuous futures) | dxFeed (needs layer 2 access) | TimeAndSale tape with aggressor side; Order-v2 indexed orders assembled into a per-price book (one tick per level, full depth) | 1m/1h/1d candles; host studies: volume profile, VPIN, large prints, bar flow |
| AAPL, TSLA (equities) | dxFeed (layer 2) | tape, 35 to 75 percent of volume has NO aggressor side; Order-v2 book | as futures; regular session only (09:30 to 16:00 New York) |
| NVDA | dxFeed (layer 2) | tape and book | not archived |

Not available anywhere: dxFeed DOM service (no entitlement), native dxFeed
candles, individual order IDs on Binance, a complete historical tick archive
(the archive keeps only the largest prints of each minute), historical depth
outside the verified August 2026 hours.

Feed vocabulary (use these exact terms in concepts): **top of book** (best
bid and ask), **aggregated depth** (size per price level, what Binance and the
assembled dxFeed book give), **individual orders** (dxFeed Order-v2 only, and
only inside the runtime's L2 engine, not exposed raw to the browser),
**tape** (individual trades with side), **candles** (OHLCV plus sided volume).

## 2. Inputs an indicator receives (`IndicatorBarCtx`)

| Field | Content | Availability |
|---|---|---|
| `bar` | OHLCV, `buy_volume`/`sell_volume` (read through `barSides`), `trade_count`, `closed`, `reconstructed`, `interval_ms`, `provider`, `marketKind`/`assetClass` | always; 1m source bars, other timeframes resampled in the browser; at most 10,080 bars retained |
| `trades` + `tradeAvailability` | the forming bar's individual prints | live bars after activation only; replayed bars get `[]` and `HISTORICAL_UNAVAILABLE` |
| `ticks` (declare `ticksWanted: true`) | the pair's print store: every live print since connection plus server history above a size floor; `coverageOf(from,to)` returns `all`, `thin` or `none` | live only |
| `liquidity` | lane-0 heatmap: 1-second columns of resting bid/ask size per price row, buy/sell flow per row, up to 64 largest prints per column; recent 20 min fine, 2 h deep (10 s columns) | live; a bounded history stream for backfill |
| `l2` (declare `category: "l2"`) | `ladder()` exact book `[price,size]`; `walls()` with evidence (`size, peak, ratio, fill, eaten, absorbing, startTs, endTs, endedBy, detail`); `events()` up to 5,000 (`wall_born/shrink/trade/hidden/end`, `trade`, `hidden`, `segment`); zoom `lanes()`; `trusted()`, `status(channel)`, `version` | live only; reads the state NOW, not as of a replayed bar; `undefined` until the first L2 frame |
| `bandHistory` | on-demand host replay of resting size and traded flow for a price band over a window (2 h, 256 rows, 100k cells per request) | layer 1 |
| `barIndex`, `barCount` | position in the series being fed | always from the runtime |
| `replace`, `replaying`, `drawNow` | forming-bar revision, replay pass, redraw request | always |

Heatmap and lane geometry: columns capped at 1,023 rows per side; lane 0
reaches about ±5 percent on BTC/ETH and ±0.87 percent on NQ; wider lanes
double row and column size.

## 3. Execution model

- Incremental: `createState(settings)` once, `onBar(ctx)` per bar event. A
  forming bar is revised with `replace: true`, possibly many times per second.
  Never recompute from a history array.
- Full replay on activation, symbol/provider/timeframe switch, reconnect, and
  any `recompute` setting change. `maxReplayBars` bounds it.
- Fault latching: a throw, a non-finite number, or a non-string color disables
  the indicator until it is removed and re-added. Fail closed to `null`.
- `ta.*` (62 streaming factories: sma, ema, rsi, macd, atr, bollinger,
  keltner, adx, superTrend, ttmSqueeze, vwap, obv, mfi, stdev, highest,
  lowest, prev, crossover, crossunder, ...) and `draw.*` handles are created
  once in `createState`, inside the guarded creation window.
- Main thread. An L2 frame costs about 0.2 ms at p99; lane columns are
  megabytes and are handed out by reference. Never copy them per bar.

## 4. Rendering an indicator can do by itself

| Primitive | Notes |
|---|---|
| Line series per output key | per-bar color via `color(state, settings)`, `recolor` for cheap cosmetic changes, `lineWidth` |
| Placement | `overlay`, `ownPane` (with fixed `priceLines` and optional `autoscaleRange`), `sharedPane` with another SDK indicator |
| `ctx.draw.fill(a, b, {colorAbove, colorBelow}, key)` | band between two values, split at sign flips; several keyed fills |
| `ctx.markers.plotshape/plotchar` | circle, square, arrowUp, arrowDown; positions aboveBar, belowBar, inBar, atPriceTop/Bottom/Middle; `text`, `size`, `color` |
| `draw.line/box/label/polyline` | created-once handles in domain coordinates, mutated per bar |
| `draw.table` | DOM overlay, top right, cell text and color |
| `getHoverData` | arbitrary hover payload, but only indicators hand-wired in ChartPanel show it today |

Not self-serve (needs a chart-pipeline change, PM approval required): custom
canvas primitives, histogram series, forward-displaced plots, attaching to
another indicator's bespoke series, hover tooltips for a new indicator,
alerts or notifications (no alert system exists in the web app at all).

Cycle-1 definition of an "alert": a documented condition rendered as a marker
and surfaced in the indicator's own table or legend. A notification channel is
a separate SDK project, not something a specialist builds inside an indicator.

## 5. Settings and presets

Field kinds: boolean, checkboxList, radioGroup, select, numeric, color,
slider, staticHint, custom. Each has `affects: recompute | cosmetic |
liveOnly`, optional `visibleIf`, and `tab: "inputs" | "visibility" |
"advanced"`. Numeric and slider `step` must be > 0. Every settings key must
appear in the schema (a test enforces it) and presets come for free. Since
2026-10-26 `tab: "advanced"` renders as a collapsed Advanced group at the end
of the Inputs tab (UX option A, merged): keep at most three primary controls
and mark the rest advanced. `recolor(oldColor, settings)` cannot reclassify
history, so a setting that changes which bars are colored or marked must be
`recompute`, not `cosmetic`.

## 6. Historical Mode policies

`OHLCV_POLICY` (candles), `CANDLE_FLOW_POLICY` (candles plus sided volume:
Binance, and ES/NQ/AAPL/TSLA through the bar-flow study), `TRADE_FAMILY_POLICY`
(host studies), capability lists including `l2_states`, `l2_walls`,
`l2_prints` (verified hours only), or an `unavailableReason` sentence. A
candle archive must never fabricate ticks or depth.

## 6b. Screenshots and the fixture runtime

Binance is unreachable from this container (runtime log: required routes
never reached CONNECTED). Screenshots therefore come from the UX specialist's
fixture WebSocket server at `PM/cycle1/screenshots/tools/fixture-ws-server.cjs`
(speaks the frame contract: selection, snapshot of synthetic closed 1m bars,
health, book, a forming bar revised each second) on port 8001, with the web
app on 8000 and the Playwright scripts beside it. Every snapshot bar must be
`closed: true` or the frame is dropped. Procedure in `PM/cycle1/ux-standard.md`
section 4.

## 7. Testing and evidence tooling

- `replayBars(def, bars, settings)` replays a bar sequence through an indicator.
- Fakes for the tick store (`AggressiveTape`, see `vwap-bands/tests`) and for
  `IndicatorL2` (hand-built objects, see `liquidity-voids/tests`).
- Vitest for the web app, jest for the runtime, mutation specs in
  `benchmarks/mutations`, `npm run gate` for everything.
- Recorded market data is NOT in the repository. The runtime can replay
  recorded days from the collector's SQLite (`replay-source.js`) only on a
  machine with that storage. Validation here therefore uses synthetic
  fixtures and whatever live Binance data this container can reach; the
  validation specialist states which.

## 8. Units

Binance sizes are base asset (documented USD notional is `price * size`);
futures are contracts; equities are shares. Never add across providers.

## 9. Data paths added in cycle 2 (2026-10-26)

- Spot endpoint overrides `TT_BINANCE_SPOT_WS_BASE_URL` and
  `TT_BINANCE_SPOT_REST_BASE_URL`: the stream transport can use Binance's
  unblocked market-data mirrors. Spot only; perpetual endpoints untouched.
- REST candle transport `TT_BINANCE_TRANSPORT=rest` (`TT_BINANCE_REST_POLL_MS`
  250..20000, `TT_BINANCE_REST_WEIGHT_BUDGET`): spot candles only, forming and
  closed, finality judged at dispatch time, catch-up and hole repair with an
  honest `history incomplete` reason; tape and book unavailable, health reason
  `Polled spot candles · no tape · no order book`. This is how the cloud
  container reaches real Binance spot data; evidence in
  `pm/cycle2/acceptance-rest-transport.md`. Stage B2 (aggregate trades behind a
  capability contract) and Stage C (depth) remain in the backlog.
- Browser-direct mode (`VITE_DATA_SOURCE=browser-binance` at build time, or
  `?source=browser-binance`): the web app reads Binance spot in the browser
  (REST history, then kline, trade and depth20 streams), no runtime. Candle and
  tick indicators work; the L2 engine family shows unavailable.
- Environment facts: the container cannot open WebSockets (proxy) and
  Binance's primary API geo-blocks it (451); the mirror answers REST with
  `access-control-allow-origin: *`.

# Validation protocol (cycle 1)

Owner: validation and performance specialist. Applies to every indicator that
reaches step 6 of the pipeline in `PM/CHARTER.md`. The harness that runs these
checks lives on the repository at `apps/web/src/lib/chart/validation/`
(import from `./validation/index.ts`); each utility has its own proving test in
`apps/web/src/lib/chart/validation/tests/`. A check is not passed because the
specialist says so; it is passed because a vitest file in the indicator's
`tests/` folder (or a validation spec next to it) runs the check and the
evidence file named in the report records the result.

Vocabulary: **replay** is the runtime's `onSnapshot`/activation pass over the
retained bars (`replaying: true`, no `trades`, `HISTORICAL_UNAVAILABLE`);
**live** is bars arriving one at a time through `onBar` as `APPENDED`, then
`REPLACED_EDGE` for every forming revision, then `REPLACED_EDGE` once more when
the bar closes. The harness classifies arrivals with the chart's own
`mergeChartBar`, so a duplicated closed bar is a `REPLACED_EDGE` and a late bar
is a `REBUILT`, exactly as `ChartPanel` would dispatch them.

## How to run the harness

```bash
export PATH=/root/.nvm/versions/node/v24.21.0/bin:$PATH
cd apps/web
npx vitest run src/lib/chart/validation          # the harness proves itself (84 tests)
npx vitest run src/lib/indicators/<folder>       # the indicator's own checks
VALIDATION_BASELINES=1 NODE_OPTIONS=--expose-gc npx vitest run src/lib/chart/validation/tests/baselines.test.ts
```

Heap numbers are meaningful only under `NODE_OPTIONS=--expose-gc` (vitest's
workers inherit it); every probe result carries `heap.gcExposed` so the evidence
says which kind it is.

| Utility | File | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `trendBars`, `rangeBars`, `compressionBreakoutBars`, `volumeSpikeBars`, `timeGapBars`, `equityUnsidedBars` | `fixtures.ts` | labelled synthetic regimes, deterministic by seed |
| `relativeVolumeEquityFixture`, `relativeVolumeCryptoFixture`, `mulberry32`, `standardNormals` | `fixtures.ts` | the frozen Relative Volume fixtures of `PM/cycle1/brief-rvol-clock.md` (15-session equity U-curve with 12 labelled anomalies and predictable slots; 12,000-bar crypto cosine day), shared by the builder and the validation loop |
| `formingRevisions`, `liveArrivals`, `duplicateClosedArrivals`, `rebuiltArrivals`, `sampledIndices` | `fixtures.ts` | arrival scripts for the live driver |
| `openLiveSession`, `driveLive`, `replayCapture`, `diffOutputs`, `diffMarkers` | `liveDrive.ts` | the real `indicatorRuntime` driven the way the chart drives it, with the rollover |
| `checkTruncationInvariance` | `truncation.ts` | check 1 |
| `checkLiveReplayEquivalence`, `checkDuplicateClosedIdempotent`, `checkRebuiltEquivalence`, `checkArrivalsAgainstReplay` | `liveEquivalence.ts` | checks 2 and 3 |
| `countMarkers`, `countMarkersOver`, `markerBudgetOf`, `judgeBudget`, `captureMarkers` | `markerBudget.ts` | check 5 |
| `whaleVersusCrowd`, `busyTapeFor`, `tapeOf`, `historyTapeOf`, `ticksOf`, `candleOf` | `syntheticTape.ts` | real `AggressiveTape`s for L1 checks and the whale-versus-crowd baseline |
| `fakeL2`, `fakeL2Unsynced`, `segmentOf`, `ladderOf`, `ladderWithWall`, `wallOf`, `wallLifecycle`, `absorbingAt`, `tradeEvent` | `fakeL2.ts` | a scripted `IndicatorL2` whose trust, segment and event-slice semantics mirror `indicatorL2Of`, `L2Store.trusted` and `EventRing.since`; `atTime` cuts the ladder, walls and events at a bar close. A wall under a cut is served from a timestamped snapshot or rebuilt from its `wall_born`/`wall_trade` events, with `absorbing` by the runtime's rule (traded into within 5 s, holding half its peak); a wall with neither is omitted, never served at its final size |
| `probeReplay`, `probeFormingBar`, `probeRollover`, `probeReplayPooled` | `perfProbe.ts` | check 6; `probeReplayPooled` is the brief's frozen method (warm-ups, pooled timed runs, `onBar` plus `readOutput` only) |

## The checks

Each check states the method, the pass criterion and how the evidence is
recorded. "Evidence file" means a vitest file committed on the indicator's
branch whose test names match the check, plus any JSON or log the test writes
(kept under `PM/log/validation/<indicator>/`).

### 1. No future data

Method. `checkTruncationInvariance(def, bars, settings)` replays the fixture
once in full, then once truncated at every index (`sampledIndices` for series
longer than 1,000 bars: every 25th index plus the last 50), and compares the
committed outputs of every line and every marker at or before the cut. Run it
on at least `compressionBreakoutBars()` and `trendBars()`; tick and book
indicators also run it with the fixture's `extras` (`ticks` from `tapeOf`, `l2`
from `fakeL2` with `atTime` cutting walls and events at the bar close).

Pass. `report.ok === true`: zero output disagreements at tolerance 0 (a
documented floating-point tolerance is acceptable only for a value computed in
a different order live versus replay, never for a different value), zero marker
disagreements, no fault. An indicator that reads `barIndex`/`barCount` to place
anything is suspect by default and must show this check passing.

Evidence. Test named `no future data: truncation invariance` in the
indicator's tests; `summarizeTruncation(report)` written to the log on failure.

### 2. Replay versus live equivalence

Method. `checkLiveReplayEquivalence(def, bars, settings, { revisionsPerBar: 5 })`
feeds every bar as five forming revisions (`closed: false`, path open through
the wicks to the close, volume growing with buy, sell and unknown volume scaled
independently through `barSides`, so an equity bar's unsided share stays
unsided) followed by the closed bar,
through the real runtime, and compares the committed outputs and markers with a
clean replay of the same bars. For a tick or book indicator supply `tradesFor`
and `extras` so the live side sees what the terminal would give it.

Pass. Either `report.equal === true`, or the indicator's doc page states the
exact difference and the test pins it: for a tick-only indicator the expected
and accepted difference is `outputsEqual === true`, replay markers `[]`, live
markers present (`largeTradeBubbles` is the reference: see
`validation/tests/liveEquivalence.test.ts`). Any output difference on a
candle-only indicator fails; it means forming revisions were committed (the
harness catches this with a running sum that ignores `replace`).

Evidence. Test named `replay versus live equivalence`; the documented
difference quoted in the indicator's doc page under "Replay and Historical
Mode".

### 3. Gap, duplicate and late-event behavior

Method, five parts, all through the live driver so the runtime's own
classification is what the indicator sees:

- Duplicate closed dispatch: `checkDuplicateClosedIdempotent(def, bars, settings, index)`.
  The duplicate must be classified `REPLACED_EDGE` and the result must equal a
  feed in which the bar arrived once.
- `REBUILT`: `checkRebuiltEquivalence(def, bars, settings, lateIndex)`. A bar
  arriving two bars late must leave the indicator equal to a clean replay
  (`sawRebuilt === true`). Module-level state (a cache keyed by instrument, a
  slot pool) is where this fails; the runtime replays, the module does not.
- Reconnect that clears the tick store: an arrival script whose `act` hook
  resets the `AggressiveTape` and hands a new `IndicatorTicks` to the session
  (`session.setTicks`). Every bar whose window the store no longer covers must
  read `null`, never a stale value (reference: `tickVolume` in the same test file).
- Out-of-order prints: `tapeOf` with prints in shuffled order must give the
  same per-window sums as in-order arrival (`syntheticTape.test.ts`); an
  indicator that keeps its own print buffer must show the same.
- Time gap: run the indicator on `timeGapBars()`; the output at
  `labels.firstBarAfterGapIndex` must follow the indicator's documented gap rule
  (restart, carry, or null), and nothing before the gap may change.
- Book indicators: run the same checks with `extras.l2` from `fakeL2`. Its
  `trusted(ts)` is false before the segment start, on a GAP or null segment and
  when `l2_walls` or `l2_events` is unsynced; `events(cursor)` is `complete`
  only for an exact cursor on a synced ring past any reported loss, exactly as
  `EventRing.since`; so an indicator's missing-event and untrusted gates can be
  exercised with `patch({ synced: { l2_events: false } })`, `missedEvents`, a
  cursor before `eventResetSeq`, or `segment: segmentOf(start)`.

Pass. All five hold; no fault; no `NaN`.

Evidence. Tests named `duplicate closed bar is idempotent`, `late bar rebuilds
to the replay`, `reconnect clears the store honestly`, `out-of-order prints`,
`time gap`.

### 4. Warm-up

Method. Replay a fixture shorter than the warm-up length and one exactly at
it; read every line of `replayCapture(...).outputs`.

Pass. Every value before the documented warm-up length is `null`; the first
non-null value appears exactly at the documented index; no value anywhere is
`NaN`, `Infinity`, or a fabricated `0` standing in for "not yet" (a genuine
zero from complete data is fine, and the test must say which it is). The
warm-up length (bars, and for tick indicators the coverage condition) is stated
in the doc page and in a `staticHint` of the settings schema.

Evidence. Test named `warm-up is null, never NaN or zero`; the doc sentence.

### 5. Marker and alert budget

Method. `countMarkers(def, bars, settings, { live })` on a busy synthetic
instrument: `trendBars({ count: 1000 })` or the breakout fixture with
`busyTapeFor(bars, { printsPerBar: 60 })` supplying trades live (whales at 0.2
percent of prints, 100 times the small size). In cycle 1 an alert is a marker
(`PM/CHARTER.md`), so this is also the alert budget. Record `per1000Bars`,
`maxPerBar`, `barsWithMarkers` and the breakdown by shape and color.

The budget carries `faulted`; a run in which the runtime latched the indicator
counts nothing and `judgeBudget` fails it whatever the rate.

Pass. Ceilings, unless the build brief sets a different one with a reason:
at most **40 markers per 1,000 bars** for a signal-type tool (a trader can act
on roughly one mark an hour on 1-minute bars), at most **200 per 1,000** for a
descriptive annotation tool (levels, labels), and at most **3 markers on one
bar**. A duplicated closed bar must not add markers (`countMarkersOver` with
`duplicateClosedArrivals`). The judged verdict is `judgeBudget(budget, ceiling,
maxPerBar).ok`.

Evidence. Test named `marker budget`; `summarizeBudget(...)` line in the log.

### 6. Performance

Method. The three probes in `perfProbe.ts`, run with `NODE_OPTIONS=--expose-gc`:

- `probeReplay(def, bars10080, settings)`: activation over 10,080 one-minute
  bars (the chart's retention cap); per-`onBar` p50 and p99, wall time of the
  whole replay, heap delta.
- `probeFormingBar(def, warmup, settings, { seconds: 60, revisionsPerSecond: 50, printsPerMinute: 1000 })`:
  one bar revised 3,000 times with 1,000 prints landing in a real tape
  (`ctx.ticks`) and in `ctx.trades` (`LIVE_COMPLETE`); per-revision p50 and p99.
- `probeRollover(def, bars10080, settings, { extraBars: 1000 })`: the chart at
  the cap, then 1,000 more bars so the front rolls off and `discardLeading`
  runs; retained output lengths, markers older than the oldest retained bar,
  heap growth per extra bar. The appended bars are built by `rolloverBar`,
  which writes sides through `barSides` (sided, buy-only and unsided bars each
  stay what they were).
- `probeReplayPooled(def, bars, settings, { warmupRuns: 3, timedRuns: 5 })`:
  the method frozen in the Relative Volume brief (amendment 9): three untimed
  warm-up replays, five timed replays, per-bar samples pooled, p99 by nearest
  rank, timed region `onBar` plus `readOutput` with no-op draw and marker
  namespaces, no runtime bookkeeping. Its `extras` go through the same
  knowledge-time cutoffs as `indicatorRuntime.makeCtx`: `liquidity.atTime(barClose)`
  with a null answer meaning unavailable, and `l2.atTime(barClose)` with a
  fallback to the uncut view. When a brief states a per-bar p99
  criterion (Relative Volume: under 0.05 ms over the first 10,080 bars of the
  crypto fixture) this is the probe that judges it; `probeReplay` remains the
  measurement of a real activation and keeps the reference rows below.

Pass. Replay p99 ≤ 0.5 ms and total replay wall ≤ 2 s for a candle indicator
(≤ 5 s for a book or tick indicator that reads the store per bar); forming
revision p99 ≤ 1 ms (an indicator revised 50 times a second gets at most 50 ms
of the main thread per second); after the rollover every output line has
exactly 10,080 entries, `markersBeforeRetention === 0`, and heap growth is
flat: `bytesPerExtraBar` within 3× the harness floor measured below (about
370 B per bar, which is the runtime's own output arrays and the bar frames
themselves). A probe that reports `faulted` fails regardless of timing.

Evidence. JSON written by the indicator's baseline test
(`VALIDATION_BASELINES_OUT`), kept under `PM/log/validation/<indicator>/perf.json`,
and the three formatted lines in the report.

Reference points measured on this container (4 cores, Intel Xeon 2.10 GHz,
Node 24.21.0, gc exposed, `trendBars({ count: 10080, driftPerBar: 0.5, noise: 25 })`,
2026-10-26, raw JSON kept at `PM/log/validation/baselines-2026-10-26.json`):

| Indicator | Replay 10,080 bars: onBar p50 / p99 / max (ms), wall (ms), heap Δ | Forming bar, 3,000 revisions, 1,000 prints: p50 / p99 / max (ms), wall (ms) | Rollover +1,000: outputs retained, p50 / p99 (ms), heap B per bar |
|---|---|---|---|
| rsi | 0.0003 / 0.0024 / 0.097, 20, 0.74 MB | 0.0002 / 0.0024 / 0.649, 29 | value 10,080; 0.0005 / 0.0046; 377 |
| bollinger | 0.0008 / 0.0043 / 0.192, 32, 1.55 MB | 0.0006 / 0.0018 / 0.058, 24 | basis, upper, lower 10,080 each; 0.0009 / 0.0050; 385 |
| cvdDivergence (registry def only, see note) | 0.0002 / 0.0004 / 0.051, 14, 0.14 MB | 0.0002 / 0.0003 / 0.028, 18 | no lines; 0.0002 / 0.0005; 361 |
| l2FeedStatus (scripted book: 1,000-level ladder, 20 walls, 2,100 events) | 0.0001 / 0.0004 / 0.146, 35, 0.18 MB | 0.0543 / 0.1702 / 1.911, 229 | no lines; 0.0884 / 0.1910; 370 |

Pooled method on the Relative Volume crypto fixture (bars 1..10,080, three
warm-ups, five timed runs, `onBar` plus `readOutput`), the floor for the
brief's criterion 4: 

| Indicator | Pooled onBar+readOutput p50 / p90 / p99 / max (ms) | Per-run p99 (ms) | Per-run wall (ms) |
|---|---|---|---|
| rsi | 0.0002 / 0.0003 / 0.0009 / 0.264 | 0.0023, 0.0007, 0.0005, 0.0005, 0.0004 | 11, 6, 6, 5, 5 |
| bollinger | 0.0003 / 0.0006 / 0.0009 / 0.271 | 0.0011, 0.0009, 0.0009, 0.0009, 0.0009 | 10, 6, 6, 6, 6 |

Raw JSON: `PM/log/validation/baselines-pooled-2026-10-26.json`. The first timed
run is still warmer-than-cold but slower than the rest (JIT settling), which is
why the brief pools five runs; a single-run p99 would be luck.

Notes on the reference points. The `cvdDivergence` registry def has an empty
`onBar` (its computation is hand-wired in `ChartPanel.tsx` through
`cvdDivergenceRenderer.ts` and `cvdDivergence.ts`), so its row is the harness
floor: what the timing wrapper and the runtime cost with no indicator work at
all. A real measurement of CVD Divergence needs the batch functions
(`rollingDeltaSeries`, `detectCvdDivergences`) timed directly, which the
indicator SDK probe cannot see. `l2FeedStatus` writes about ten table cells per
revision and reads the whole ladder and event list each time, which is why its
forming-bar cost is 200 times the floor while still inside the 1 ms ceiling.
The rollover heap slope of 361 to 385 B per bar is the same for all four, so it
is the runtime and the bar frames, not the indicators; an indicator's own
retained state shows as a slope above that (the harness proves this with a
`hoarder` def in `perfProbe.test.ts`).

### 7. Baseline comparison

For the cycle-1 indicator the brief freezes the fixtures and the acceptance
numbers: `relativeVolumeEquityFixture()` (labels `anomalyIndices`,
`predictableSlotIndices`, `evaluationIndices`, `sessionStarts`) and its
`{ flatSeasonality: true }` control, judged by the recall and false-positive
bounds in `PM/cycle1/brief-rvol-clock.md`; the fixture's own proving test is
`validation/tests/relativeVolumeFixtures.test.ts`. The general rule follows.

Method. The build brief names the simpler baseline (a rolling average, plain
RVOL, a raw imbalance line, a candle-only version of the same idea). The
specialist implements the baseline as a `RuntimeIndicatorDef` in the
indicator's `tests/` (or Codex does, gate G4) and runs both on labelled
fixtures:

- Descriptive tools are judged on **detection accuracy on labelled fixtures**:
  for `compressionBreakoutBars` the detection index versus
  `labels.breakoutIndex` (hit if within the documented tolerance, early if
  before, late if after, plus false positives inside `compressionStart..breakoutIndex`);
  for `volumeSpikeBars` the same against `labels.spikeIndex`; for
  `rangeBars` and `trendBars` the false-positive count. Run each fixture over at
  least 10 seeds and report hits, early, late and false positives for the tool
  and the baseline side by side.
- L1 tools must pass the **whale-versus-crowd** fixture: `whaleVersusCrowd()`
  builds two tapes making the identical candle (print count withheld); the tool
  must respond differently to the two tapes while the candle-only baseline, by
  construction, cannot. `largeTradeBubbles` is the reference (one bubble on the
  whale tape, none on the crowd tape, `syntheticTape.test.ts`).
- L2 tools state what is **observed** (ladder, walls, events as served) versus
  **inferred**, and the baseline comparison is run on the same `fakeL2` script so
  the book is the same for both.

Pass. The report states, in one sentence, what the tool shows that the baseline
hides, and the table shows it: strictly fewer false positives at equal or
better hits, or hits the baseline cannot produce (whale versus crowd). A tool
that only matches the baseline is returned to the backlog.

Evidence. Test named `beats the baseline on labelled fixtures`; the
side-by-side table in the report.

### 8. Predictive claims

Method. Any claim of the form "after X, price tends to Y" is predictive. The
specialist freezes the rule (parameters included) on a development period,
then measures it on an **unseen period** chosen after the rule was frozen:
a different seed range of the synthetic fixtures (declared in the test before
the development runs are made), and, where this container can reach it, a live
Binance stretch recorded after the rule was written. Forward observation only:
the outcome window starts at the signal bar's close and uses no bar before it.

Overfitting rule. Choosing the best of several parameter sets, thresholds or
variants by their result on the development period and reporting that result
is rejected. At most **one** rule is carried to the unseen period; if it fails
there the claim is dropped, not re-tuned. Every variant tried is listed in the
report with its development result so the selection is visible.

Pass. The unseen-period result is reported with its sample size and with the
same statistic on the baseline; a claim with fewer than 30 signals in the
unseen period is reported as "insufficient evidence", not as a pass. Marketing
copy may state only what the unseen period supports.

Evidence. `PM/EXPERIMENTS.md` entry with the frozen rule, the declared unseen
period, every variant, and the two results.

### 9. Equity unsided volume

Method. Run the indicator on `equityUnsidedBars()` (dxFeed equity identity;
every third bar carries no side, the others carry both sides and a 40 percent
unsided remainder) and read every output that depends on sides.

Pass. On `labels.unsidedIndices` every side-dependent output is `null`
(`barSides(bar).known === false` draws nothing, never zero). On
`labels.partiallySidedIndices` the delta equals `buy - sell` from `barSides`
and the unsided remainder is **not** counted as selling; where the tool shows a
delta on equities it shows `unsidedShare` beside it or in the doc. The code
reads sides only through `barSides`: `grep -n "volume - " <folder>` and
`grep -n "buy_volume" <folder>` outside `barSides` calls must be empty. No gate
on `provider === "..."` except to label units.

Evidence. Test named `equity unsided volume is honest`.

### 10. Historical Mode policy honesty

Method. Read the indicator's `HISTORICAL_INDICATOR_POLICIES` entry and the data
it actually reads in `onBar`:

| Reads | Required policy |
|---|---|
| candles only (`bar` OHLCV) | `OHLCV_POLICY` |
| `barSides` / `buy_volume` | `CANDLE_FLOW_POLICY` |
| `ctx.ticks`, `ctx.trades` | `unavailableReason` (or `l2_prints` only if the doc proves the verified hours suffice) |
| `ctx.l2`, `ctx.liquidity`, `bandHistory` | `l2_states` / `l2_walls` capabilities or `unavailableReason` |
| host studies | `TRADE_FAMILY_POLICY` |

Then replay the indicator with `replayCapture` and no extras (what a candle
archive gives): a tick or book indicator must produce `null` outputs and no
markers (`largeTradeBubbles` and the `tickVolume` reference pass this); a
candle-flow indicator on bars without `buy_volume` must produce `null`.

Pass. The policy row matches the table; the replay-without-extras result is
honest nulls; the doc page's "Historical Mode" paragraph says the same thing as
the policy; `historicalIndicatorAvailability(id, capabilities)` for the
instrument's real capability set agrees with the doc's claim of where the tool
works. A policy that promises Historical Mode for data the archive does not
contain fails the indicator outright.

Evidence. Test named `Historical Mode policy is honest` (asserting the policy
entry and the null replay).

## Report template

One report per validation loop, at `PM/log/validation/<indicator>/loop-<n>.md`.
Codex's G3 challenge list is answered item by item in the same file.

```markdown
# Validation report: <indicator id> loop <n>

Branch: <branch>   Commit: <sha>   Validator: <name>   Date: <date>
Commands run: <each command with pass/fail counts>

| # | Check | Result | Evidence file (test name) | Measured | Required fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | No future data | PASS / FAIL | <tests/...test.ts> (<test name>) | cuts checked, violations | |
| 2 | Replay vs live | PASS / PASS WITH DOCUMENTED DIFFERENCE / FAIL | | output diffs, marker diffs | |
| 3 | Gap, duplicate, late | PASS / FAIL | | per part | |
| 4 | Warm-up | PASS / FAIL | | warm-up length | |
| 5 | Marker budget | PASS / FAIL | | per 1,000 bars, max per bar, ceiling | |
| 6 | Performance | PASS / FAIL | perf.json | replay p50/p99, forming p50/p99, rollover B/bar | |
| 7 | Baseline comparison | PASS / FAIL | | hits/early/late/false positives, tool vs baseline | |
| 8 | Predictive claims | PASS / INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE / N/A / FAIL | EXPERIMENTS.md entry | unseen-period statistic, n | |
| 9 | Equity unsided | PASS / FAIL / N/A | | | |
| 10 | Historical policy | PASS / FAIL | | policy row, null replay | |

Codex G3 challenge items: <item> -> <answer with evidence>

Verdict: ACCEPT / FIX AND RERUN (list) / RETURN TO BACKLOG (reason)
Not verified: <anything the container could not reach, e.g. live rendering, real dxFeed data>
```

A check marked FAIL always carries a required fix that names the file and the
behavior, never "investigate". Two loops are the budget; a third failure
returns the concept to the backlog with this report attached.
# UX standard for new indicators (cycle 1)

Written by the UX seat from the code in `Terminal-L1-L2-development-indicator-sdk`
(snapshot 2026-10-26) and from rendered charts (`PM/cycle1/screenshots/`).
Code wins over this page; report a disagreement to the PM. The rules in
part 2 are what every new indicator must follow; part 1 is what the house
already does, so a new indicator looks like it belongs.

## 1. House conventions observed

### 1.1 Colours

| Where | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Live chart (default, light) | background `#f2efe6`, up candle `#f8f8f5` with `#111111` stroke, down candle `#111111` | `CHART_PALETTE`, `ChartPanel.tsx` |
| Dark chart (Historical Mode, `.shell.historical-active`) | background `#111316`, up `#36d684`, down `#f16a72`, text `#9ca3af` | `DARK_CHART_PALETTE`, `App.css` |
| UI tokens | `--bull #4ade80`, `--bear #f87171`, `--warn #fbbf24` (dark page); `--bull #0e9a66`, `--bear #c23854`, `--warn #b07a1e`, `--accent #0e9a93` (light page) | `index.css` |
| Existing indicator lines | RSI `#4d3d78`; SMA purple `#8672b3`, blue `#2563eb`, green `#16a34a`; CVD up `#16a34a`, down `#dc2626`; CVD on dark `#facc15`; reference lines `rgba(10,10,10,0.3)` | `rsi.ts`, `sma.ts`, `INDICATOR_PALETTE` |
| Marker default | `#787b86`, shapes `circle`, `square`, `arrowUp`, `arrowDown` | `draw/markers.ts` |
| Table overlay | `rgba(10,10,10,0.82)` background, `#f8f8f5` text, mono 11 px, top-right (or bottom-right) | `.draw-table-overlay`, `draw/table.ts` |

The candles are monochrome on the default chart, so green and red are free
for meaning: buyers, sellers, bull, bear. Hand-wired lines use
`INDICATOR_LINE_WIDTH = 2`; an SDK line takes `lines[].lineWidth` and falls
back to 2. SDK line series are created with `lastValueVisible: false` and
`priceLineVisible: false`, so an SDK line has no current-value label on the
price axis; own-pane `priceLines` do get dotted 1 px lines with axis labels
(`ChartPanel.tsx`, runtime series creation).

### 1.2 Legend rows

Top-left stack over the chart, one white pill per indicator
(`IndicatorLegendRow`): mono 11 px bold label, an optional grey `detail`, and
eye / gear / trash buttons that appear on hover or focus. In Historical Mode
the pill is dark. The label is the registry `label` (`"RSI (14)"`, so the
default parameter is in the name). The detail is set per indicator by hand
in `MainPanel.tsx`; a generic SDK indicator with a `settingsSchema` gets label
and gear only. Observed detail strings: `Swing 20`, `≥ 5 ct`,
`Bar volume × typical price`, `Live only · 20 bars required`,
`Warming · ≥ 0.50z`, ` · history unavailable`, `waiting for the book`,
`reconnecting`, `loading the view's prints`.

### 1.3 Settings dialog

`IndicatorSettingsModal` is a draggable fixed panel about 320 px wide, title
bar with the indicator label and a close button, body, footer with
**Reset to default**, **Defaults ▾** (presets) and **Ok**. Edits apply live;
Ok only closes. The generic body (`GenericIndicatorSettingsBody`) stacks
fields vertically, label above control; sliders show the value inline
(`Inner band (σ) 1.00`); tabs **Inputs** / **Visibility** appear only when a
field declares `tab`. Hand-built dialogs (Aggressive Orders, the heatmap)
copy the same tab strip and classes. Every dialog has presets for free from
its schema (`docs/architecture/indicators/setting-presets.md`).

### 1.4 Copy tone

Sentence case labels with the unit in brackets: `Session length`,
`Lookback (bars)`, `Show orders larger than (BTC)`. Hints are plain full
sentences that say what the control does and what it costs:
"Changing the anchor, the session length or a band width replays every bar."
Legend details and status strings are short lowercase phrases after ` · `.
Status chips: `LIVE`, `SYNCING`, `STALE`, `QUIET`, `HISTORY ONLY`,
`Feed unavailable`, `Live profile catching up`, `VPIN waiting for trades`,
`Analytics incomplete — reload to rebuild`. Numbers are mono and tabular.

### 1.5 How indicators signal warm-up, degraded and unavailable today

- An SDK indicator returns `null` from `readOutput` until it has enough bars:
  the line simply starts later (RSI draws nothing for its first 14 bars).
  Nothing tells the trader it is warming up.
- Hand-wired indicators put state in the legend detail (`Warming ·`,
  `Unavailable ·`, `history unavailable`, `waiting for the book`) or in a
  status chip. The data rule is "warm-up failure is not an empty signal";
  never draw a zero or a flat line for data you do not have
  (`data-for-indicator-authors.md`, Rules).
- A throw, a non-finite number or a non-string colour latches a fault: the
  indicator goes blank until removed and re-added, with no message in the
  legend. Fail closed to `null` instead.
- Historical Mode greys out indicators whose policy says unavailable and
  shows the `unavailableReason` sentence.

## 2. The standard every new indicator follows

### 2.1 Default view: readable in three seconds

1. One question, one primary mark. The first thing on screen is the series,
   band or marker that answers the indicator's question. Secondary context
   (a baseline, a threshold) is thinner and lighter.
2. At most two lines and one fill at default settings. A third series goes
   behind a Visibility toggle, off by default.
3. Pane or overlay: a value in price units (a level, a band, a VWAP) is an
   `overlay`. A bounded ratio, score or oscillator is an `ownPane` with
   `priceLines` at its decision levels and a fixed `autoscaleRange`, so the
   pane does not rescale as the trader scrolls. Do not share a pane unless
   the two indicators are read together.
4. Line widths: primary 2, secondary 1. Fills between 0.12 and 0.25 alpha.
5. It must read on both charts: the cream live chart and the dark Historical
   chart. Take a screenshot of each before handing over (section 4).
6. The registry `label` carries the default parameter, as `RSI (14)` does,
   so the legend explains itself.

### 2.2 Controls: at most three primary, the rest Advanced

- Inputs tab: at most three controls without a `tab` (or `tab: "inputs"`).
  These are the ones a trader changes in the first session: the length,
  the threshold, the mode.
- Everything else that changes the calculation: `tab: "advanced"`. The
  generic dialog renders it as a closed **Advanced** group at the end of the
  Inputs tab (prototype on the UX branch, see
  `PM/cycle1/advanced-settings-proposal.md`; until merged, keep the list of
  advanced keys in the README and leave them off the schema's first screen).
- Colours, show/hide toggles, opacity, labels: `tab: "visibility"`.
- Prefer `slider` or `select` with named stops over free `numeric` boxes;
  a numeric field needs `step > 0` or the generic dialog refuses every value.
- One explanatory paragraph at most on the Inputs tab; a one-line hint per
  control is fine. The full explanation lives in the indicator's README.
- Use the generic dialog. A hand-built dialog needs a PM decision.
- Settings that depend on the instrument (a size floor) use
  `settingsFieldsFor` for the suggested value, never a provider branch in
  the schema.

### 2.3 Colour semantics

- Green/red (`--bull`/`--bear`, or the candle-up/down colours on the dark
  chart) only for direction or side: buying vs selling, above vs below.
- A non-directional series takes one neutral colour from the house set:
  purple `#4d3d78` / `#8672b3`, blue `#2563eb`, amber `#facc15` (dark chart
  only). Never introduce a new hue for a single indicator.
- Thresholds and reference lines: `rgba(10,10,10,0.3)` on the cream chart,
  `rgba(250,250,247,0.32)` on the dark one.
- Warning or "degraded" state: `--warn`, never red (red means sell).
- Expose a colour in Visibility only when the trader could plausibly need
  to change it; the default must already be right.

### 2.4 Markers

- At most two event types per indicator, one shape each: `arrowUp` below the
  bar and `arrowDown` above it for directional events, `circle` for neutral.
- `text` at most three characters; prefer none.
- Density: at default settings on a 1m BTC chart, no more than about one
  marker per ten bars on average. If the fixture shows more, raise the
  default threshold; the trader can lower it under Advanced.
- Marker colour equals the colour of the line or side it belongs to.
- Markers never replace the line: a marker says "here", the series says "why".

### 2.5 "Alert conditions" without an alert system

There is no notification channel. An alert condition is:

1. a named, documented condition in the README under **Alert conditions**
   (name, exact rule, which bar it fires on, how often it fires on the
   fixture);
2. a marker on the bar where it fires (section 2.4);
3. a row in the indicator's own `draw.table` (top-right): condition name,
   the bar time and the value, for example `Squeeze fired · 12:34 · 1.8σ`.
   A generic SDK indicator cannot write to the legend detail today, so the
   table is the place.

Never promise a notification in copy. Use the condition's name
("Squeeze fired"), not the word "alert", in anything the trader reads.

### 2.6 First use: before warm-up completes

- Draw nothing for bars without a value (`null`), never a zero or a flat
  line, never a partial window as if it were full.
- While warming up, the table shows one row: `Warming up · 37 of 200 bars`.
  When data the indicator needs is not there (ticks in replay, L2 before
  the first frame, a timeframe outside the gate), the row says what it needs
  in the trader's terms: `Needs live trades`, `Needs 1m to 15m`,
  `Waiting for the book`. Remove the row once the indicator is live.
- State the warm-up length in the README and keep it under the chart's
  retention (`maxReplayBars`, at most 10,080 1m bars).
- Replay honesty: a value the replay cannot compute (tick-only inputs) is
  `null`, and the README says that history looks different from live.

### 2.7 Tooltips and hover: what can and cannot be promised

Only hand-wired indicators have hover today: `fvgDelta` and `sessionLevels`
through `getHoverData` in `ChartPanel.tsx`, and the Aggressive Orders bubbles
and the heatmap through their own primitives. A new SDK indicator gets no
tooltip without a chart-pipeline change and PM approval. Therefore:

- Design so nothing essential needs a hover: an SDK line has no price-axis
  value label, so the current value and the state go in the table, and
  thresholds are drawn as `priceLines` (which do get axis labels).
- You may implement `getHoverData` for later wiring, but the README must not
  claim a tooltip, and the screenshots must not show one.

### 2.8 Copy for labels and hints

- Labels: sentence case, at most four words, unit in brackets:
  `Lookback (bars)`, `Minimum size (BTC)`.
- Hints: one or two sentences, present tense, say what changes and whether
  it replays ("Replays every bar").
- Use the feed vocabulary from `CAPABILITIES.md`: top of book, aggregated
  depth, tape, candles. Say "estimate" when it is one; never "detects" or
  "predicts" unless validation established it.
- Legend detail and table cells: at most 30 characters, ` · ` between parts,
  numbers tabular.
- Status phrases are lowercase and concrete: `waiting for the book`, not
  `Initializing…`.

## 3. Handover checklist (UX)

- [ ] Default view screenshots at two zoom levels, cream and dark chart
- [ ] Inputs tab shows at most three controls; Advanced group holds the rest
- [ ] Every settings key is in the schema; presets work (test enforces)
- [ ] Warm-up row visible on first use; nothing drawn as zero
- [ ] Markers under the density cap on the fixture
- [ ] README has: question answered, default view, controls, alert
      conditions, warm-up length, what replay cannot show, no tooltip claim

## 4. Producing screenshots in this container

Binance is not reachable from the container (the runtime's startup fails
after 30 s: `binance_connection.route_error` for every route, then
`lifecycle.start_failed reason market data server: required routes never
reached CONNECTED within 30000ms`). Use the fixture runtime, which speaks
the frame contract and renders the real web app:

```sh
export PATH=/root/.nvm/versions/node/v24.21.0/bin:$PATH
cd <your worktree>                       # with node_modules linked as in the brief
cp -n .env.example .env
# 1. fixture runtime on 8001 (the Vite proxy's default target)
REPO=/home/user/Terminal-L1-L2/Terminal-L1-L2-development-indicator-sdk \
  node /home/user/Terminal-L1-L2/PM/cycle1/screenshots/tools/fixture-ws-server.cjs 8001 900 7 &
# 2. web app on 8000
npm run dev --workspace web &
# 3. screenshots: default, <indicator> zoomed out/in, <dialog indicator> Inputs/Visibility
node /home/user/Terminal-L1-L2/PM/cycle1/screenshots/tools/screenshot.cjs \
  /home/user/Terminal-L1-L2/PM/cycle1/screenshots/<indicator> "<legend label>" "<legend label with dialog>"
```

The fixture sends `selection`, `snapshot` (900 synthetic closed 1m BTC-USDT
spot bars, a random walk with slow regimes), `health` READY, a two-level
`book`, then a forming `bar` revised every second. Snapshot bars must all be
`closed: true` and on the minute, or the browser drops the frame
(`parseSnapshotFrame` in `types/frames.ts`). Tick-, L2- and heatmap-driven
indicators get no data from it; extend the fixture with `trade` and
`liquidity` frames from `frames.ts` when you need them, or state that the
screenshot is OHLCV-only. For the dark chart, switch to Historical Mode in
the UI (needs layer 1) or set `data-theme="dark"` on `<html>` in the script.
Zoom is done with the mouse wheel over `.chart-mount`; the legend row's
gear opens the dialog; `getByRole("tab", { name: "Visibility" })` switches
tabs. Playwright and Chromium are preinstalled; never run
`playwright install`. Stop both servers when done.

exec
/bin/bash -lc 'git show build/size-split-delta:apps/web/src/lib/indicators/size-split-delta/sizeSplitDeltaMath.ts' in /home/user/Terminal-L1-L2/Terminal-L1-L2-development-indicator-sdk
 succeeded in 0ms:
import type { BarFrame } from "../../../types/frames";
import type { AggressivePrint } from "../aggressive-orders/aggressiveTape";
import { firstPrintAtOrAfter, type IndicatorTicks, type TickCoverage } from "../../chart/indicatorTicks";
import { nyLocalMinuteOfDay } from "../killzones/killzones";

/**
 * Size-Split Delta: the pure, incremental machinery
 * (`pm/cycle2/brief-size-split-delta.md`, rules 1 to 13 and the round-3
 * amendments).
 *
 * Every bar is a window `[ts_open, ts_open + interval_ms)` over the terminal's
 * print store. A print at or above the bar's tier threshold is "large", the
 * rest "small"; the signed sizes of each tier are the bar's two contributions,
 * and the two lines are their cumulative sums since the anchor. The threshold
 * is read once, at the bar's first dispatch, from a ring of the last 4,096
 * sizes of committed, completely covered windows (nearest-rank p98, 200 sizes
 * minimum), or is a fixed size.
 *
 * Commits are keyed to the bar close and tail-only. An explicit closed
 * dispatch rescans the window from the store; a supersession (the next bar
 * arrives first) commits the tail's last revision values without a rescan,
 * because the runtime cannot republish the previous bar. After commit nothing
 * about the bar changes until a rebuild replays everything from the store.
 *
 * Coverage from the store is the only gate on tick-derived values; a window
 * that is not `"all"` has no value. The tape share (print volume over candle
 * volume) must be known and inside `[minTapeShare, MAX_TAPE_SHARE]`, else the
 * bar is ineligible; eligibility is frozen with the flow at commit.
 */

export const SIZE_RING_CAPACITY = 4_096;
export const TIER_MIN_SIZES = 200;
export const TIER_QUANTILE = 0.98;
export const MAX_TAPE_SHARE = 1.25;
export const RECORD_RING_CAPACITY = 10_080;
export const DAY_MS = 86_400_000;
export const WEEK_MS = 7 * DAY_MS;
export const SIZE_SPLIT_DEFAULT_K = 20;
export const SIZE_SPLIT_DEFAULT_M = 2;
export const SIZE_SPLIT_DEFAULT_Q = 2;
export const SIZE_SPLIT_DEFAULT_MIN_TAPE_SHARE = 0.5;
export const SIZE_SPLIT_DEFAULT_TIER_SIZE = 0.5;

export type TierMode = "auto" | "fixed";
export type SizeSplitAnchor = "day" | "week" | "tape";

export interface SizeSplitMathSettings {
  tierMode: TierMode;
  tierSize: number;
  anchor: SizeSplitAnchor;
  K: number;
  m: number;
  q: number;
  minTapeShare: number;
}

/** `"noStore"` is a ctx without a print store (the REST transport, a replay with no extras). */
export type SizeSplitCoverage = TickCoverage | "noStore";

export type SizeSplitStatus = "noFeed" | "tapeStarting" | "thinned" | "learning" | "shareUnknown" | "shareMismatch" | "shareLow" | "gap" | "forming" | "ready";

export type ConfirmationState = "confirms" | "lags" | "noExtreme";

export type DivergenceDirection = "bearish" | "bullish";

export interface SizeSplitCondition {
  kind: "divergence" | "conflict";
  direction: DivergenceDirection | null;
  tsOpen: number;
  /** `large_t - large_h` for a divergence; the two sums for a conflict. */
  values: readonly number[];
}

/** The tail bar's record; `sizes` is the last revision's valid sizes (newest 4,096), dropped at commit. */
export interface SizeSplitRecord {
  tsOpen: number;
  windowEnd: number;
  close: number;
  volume: number;
  tierAtOpen: number | null;
  coverage: SizeSplitCoverage;
  /** Coverage `"none"` only because the live recording began inside this window: the next bar is covered (UX finding 2). */
  tapeStarting: boolean;
  largeBar: number | null;
  smallBar: number | null;
  tapeVolume: number;
  printCount: number;
  share: number | null;
  eligible: boolean;
  /** Coverage `"all"`, a threshold and an eligible share: the bar has a value. */
  valid: boolean;
  sizes: number[] | null;
  committed: boolean;
  /** Committed by an explicit close (final styling); false while forming or when superseded. */
  confirmed: boolean;
  /** Frozen at commit so a later dispatch of the same bar reads the same thing. */
  reading: SizeSplitReading | null;
}

/** What the engine keeps per committed bar: one small struct, bounded to `RECORD_RING_CAPACITY`. */
export interface CommittedBar {
  tsOpen: number;
  tierAtOpen: number | null;
  largeBar: number | null;
  smallBar: number | null;
  share: number | null;
  valid: boolean;
  confirmed: boolean;
}

export interface SizeSplitReading {
  tsOpen: number;
  large: number | null;
  small: number | null;
  largeBar: number | null;
  smallBar: number | null;
  share: number | null;
  tier: number | null;
  sizesLearned: number;
  status: SizeSplitStatus;
  gapBars: number;
  /** Committed by a close or a supersession; provisional styling ends here (UX finding 5). */
  committed: boolean;
  confirmed: boolean;
  valid: boolean;
  /** Valid committed bars in the marker ring (at most `K`). */
  ringLength: number;
  state: ConfirmationState | null;
  divergence: DivergenceDirection | null;
  depth: number | null;
  /** The divergence condition at this bar, drawn or not. */
  divergenceHeld: DivergenceDirection | null;
  conflictHeld: boolean;
  conflictOnset: boolean;
  conflictSums: readonly [number, number] | null;
  /** The newest divergence in this anchor window, this bar's own included; the def shows it only while its toggle is on. */
  lastDivergence: SizeSplitCondition | null;
  /** The newest conflict onset in this anchor window, this bar's own included. */
  lastConflict: SizeSplitCondition | null;
}

export interface MarkerRingEntry {
  close: number;
  largeBar: number;
  smallBar: number;
  large: number;
}

export interface WindowScan {
  coverage: SizeSplitCoverage;
  largeBar: number | null;
  smallBar: number | null;
  tapeVolume: number;
  printCount: number;
  sizes: number[];
}

function isFinitePositive(value: number): boolean {
  return Number.isFinite(value) && value > 0;
}

/** The tape share of a bar, null when the candle volume is missing or not positive (fail closed). */
export function tapeShareOf(tapeVolume: number, volume: number): number | null {
  if (!Number.isFinite(volume) || volume <= 0 || !Number.isFinite(tapeVolume) || tapeVolume < 0) return null;
  return tapeVolume / volume;
}

export function shareEligible(share: number | null, minTapeShare: number): boolean {
  return share !== null && Number.isFinite(share) && share >= minTapeShare && share <= MAX_TAPE_SHARE;
}

/**
 * The window `[fromTs, toTs)` scanned once from the store: valid prints only,
 * `size >= tier` large with the boundary print included, buys positive. With
 * no threshold nothing is classified, but the valid sizes are still collected
 * so they can train the ring (amendment 3). The size list is bounded to the
 * newest `SIZE_RING_CAPACITY` (amendment 1).
 */
export function scanWindow(ticks: IndicatorTicks | undefined, fromTs: number, toTs: number, tier: number | null): WindowScan {
  if (ticks === undefined) return { coverage: "noStore", largeBar: null, smallBar: null, tapeVolume: 0, printCount: 0, sizes: [] };
  const coverage = ticks.coverageOf(fromTs, toTs);
  const prints: readonly AggressivePrint[] = ticks.prints;
  let largeBar = 0;
  let smallBar = 0;
  let tapeVolume = 0;
  let printCount = 0;
  const sizes: number[] = [];
  for (let index = firstPrintAtOrAfter(prints, fromTs); index < prints.length; index += 1) {
    const print = prints[index];
    if (print.ts >= toTs) break;
    if (!isFinitePositive(print.size) || !isFinitePositive(print.price)) continue;
    printCount += 1;
    tapeVolume += print.size;
    sizes.push(print.size);
    if (tier === null) continue;
    const signed = print.side === "buy" ? print.size : -print.size;
    if (print.size >= tier) largeBar += signed;
    else smallBar += signed;
  }
  if (sizes.length > SIZE_RING_CAPACITY) sizes.splice(0, sizes.length - SIZE_RING_CAPACITY);
  return { coverage, largeBar: tier === null ? null : largeBar, smallBar: tier === null ? null : smallBar, tapeVolume, printCount, sizes };
}

/**
 * The size ring: the last `SIZE_RING_CAPACITY` sizes in arrival order, plus the
 * same values kept sorted so the nearest-rank quantile is one index read. Both
 * are typed arrays; an insert or an eviction moves at most 4,096 doubles with
 * `copyWithin`, so a commit of a hundred prints costs well under a millisecond
 * and nothing is ever re-sorted.
 */
export interface SizeRing {
  readonly count: number;
  push(sizes: readonly number[]): void;
  /** Nearest-rank p98 of the held sizes, null under `TIER_MIN_SIZES`. */
  threshold(): number | null;
  /** The held sizes in arrival order (oldest first); for tests. */
  values(): number[];
}

export function createSizeRing(capacity = SIZE_RING_CAPACITY, minSizes = TIER_MIN_SIZES, quantile = TIER_QUANTILE): SizeRing {
  const arrival = new Float64Array(capacity);
  const sorted = new Float64Array(capacity);
  let head = 0;
  let count = 0;

  function lowerBound(value: number): number {
    let low = 0;
    let high = count;
    while (low < high) {
      const middle = (low + high) >> 1;
      if (sorted[middle] < value) low = middle + 1;
      else high = middle;
    }
    return low;
  }

  function removeSorted(value: number): void {
    const index = lowerBound(value);
    sorted.copyWithin(index, index + 1, count);
    count -= 1;
  }

  function insertSorted(value: number): void {
    const index = lowerBound(value);
    sorted.copyWithin(index + 1, index, count);
    sorted[index] = value;
    count += 1;
  }

  return {
    get count() {
      return count;
    },
    push(sizes) {
      for (const size of sizes) {
        if (!isFinitePositive(size)) continue;
        if (count === capacity) removeSorted(arrival[head]);
        arrival[head] = size;
        head = (head + 1) % capacity;
        insertSorted(size);
      }
    },
    threshold() {
      if (count < minSizes) return null;
      const index = Math.min(count - 1, Math.max(0, Math.ceil(quantile * count) - 1));
      return sorted[index];
    },
    values() {
      const out: number[] = [];
      const start = count === capacity ? head : 0;
      for (let offset = 0; offset < count; offset += 1) out.push(arrival[(start + offset) % capacity]);
      return out;
    },
  };
}

/** The anchor window a bar belongs to: UTC-aligned day or week (epoch-aligned, as the session kernel), or null for `tape`. */
export function anchorStartFor(tsOpen: number, anchor: SizeSplitAnchor): number | null {
  if (anchor === "tape") return null;
  const period = anchor === "day" ? DAY_MS : WEEK_MS;
  if (!Number.isFinite(tsOpen)) return null;
  return Math.floor(tsOpen / period) * period;
}

export interface MarkerEvaluation {
  state: ConfirmationState | null;
  /** The divergence condition at this bar. */
  divergenceHeld: DivergenceDirection | null;
  /** The drawn arrow: the condition's onset, held at this bar and not held in the same direction at the previous valid bar (PM amendment to rule 11). */
  divergence: DivergenceDirection | null;
  depth: number | null;
  conflictHeld: boolean;
  conflictOnset: boolean;
  conflictSums: readonly [number, number] | null;
}

const NO_EVALUATION: MarkerEvaluation = Object.freeze({ state: null, divergenceHeld: null, divergence: null, depth: null, conflictHeld: false, conflictOnset: false, conflictSums: null });

/**
 * Rule 11 and amendment 2 for bar `t` against a full ring of `K` valid committed
 * bars immediately before it: `h` the ring bar with the highest close (latest
 * if tied), `l` the lowest; a strict new high confirms when `large_t >
 * large_h`, lags otherwise, and draws the bearish arrow when `large_t <=
 * large_h - m · mabsL`; the mirror for a strict new low. The conflict holds
 * when the ring-plus-`t` sums of the two tiers oppose each other and each
 * clears `q · sqrt(K + 1)` times its tier's mean absolute contribution. Both
 * markers fire on the onset only: the condition holds at `t` and did not hold
 * (in that direction) at the previous valid bar, so a trend where every bar is
 * a new extreme with a lagging large tier marks once and again only after the
 * condition has cleared (PM amendment to rule 11).
 */
export function evaluateMarkers(
  ring: readonly MarkerRingEntry[],
  bar: { close: number; largeBar: number; smallBar: number; large: number },
  previous: { conflict: boolean; divergence: DivergenceDirection | null },
  settings: Pick<SizeSplitMathSettings, "K" | "m" | "q">,
): MarkerEvaluation {
  const K = settings.K;
  if (ring.length < K) return NO_EVALUATION;
  let h = 0;
  let l = 0;
  let sumAbsLarge = 0;
  let sumAbsSmall = 0;
  let sumLarge = bar.largeBar;
  let sumSmall = bar.smallBar;
  for (let index = 0; index < ring.length; index += 1) {
    const entry = ring[index];
    if (entry.close >= ring[h].close) h = index;
    if (entry.close <= ring[l].close) l = index;
    sumAbsLarge += Math.abs(entry.largeBar);
    sumAbsSmall += Math.abs(entry.smallBar);
    sumLarge += entry.largeBar;
    sumSmall += entry.smallBar;
  }
  const mabsL = sumAbsLarge / ring.length;
  const mabsS = sumAbsSmall / ring.length;
  let state: ConfirmationState = "noExtreme";
  let divergenceHeld: DivergenceDirection | null = null;
  let depth: number | null = null;
  if (bar.close > ring[h].close) {
    state = bar.large > ring[h].large ? "confirms" : "lags";
    if (bar.large <= ring[h].large - settings.m * mabsL) {
      divergenceHeld = "bearish";
      depth = bar.large - ring[h].large;
    }
  } else if (bar.close < ring[l].close) {
    state = bar.large < ring[l].large ? "confirms" : "lags";
    if (bar.large >= ring[l].large + settings.m * mabsL) {
      divergenceHeld = "bullish";
      depth = bar.large - ring[l].large;
    }
  }
  const divergence = divergenceHeld !== null && divergenceHeld !== previous.divergence ? divergenceHeld : null;
  const strength = settings.q * Math.sqrt(K + 1);
  const conflictHeld = Math.sign(sumLarge) * Math.sign(sumSmall) < 0 && Math.abs(sumLarge) >= strength * mabsL && Math.abs(sumSmall) >= strength * mabsS;
  return { state, divergenceHeld, divergence, depth: divergence === null ? null : depth, conflictHeld, conflictOnset: conflictHeld && !previous.conflict, conflictSums: [sumLarge, sumSmall] };
}

/** Status precedence, first match wins (rule 12, amendment 5). */
export function statusOf(record: Pick<SizeSplitRecord, "coverage" | "tapeStarting" | "tierAtOpen" | "share" | "eligible" | "committed">, gapBars: number, validSinceGap: number, K: number): SizeSplitStatus {
  if (record.coverage === "none" && record.tapeStarting) return "tapeStarting";
  if (record.coverage === "noStore" || record.coverage === "none") return "noFeed";
  if (record.coverage === "thin") return "thinned";
  if (record.tierAtOpen === null) return "learning";
  if (record.share === null) return "shareUnknown";
  if (record.share > MAX_TAPE_SHARE) return "shareMismatch";
  if (!record.eligible) return "shareLow";
  if (gapBars > 0 && validSinceGap < K) return "gap";
  if (!record.committed) return "forming";
  return "ready";
}

export interface SizeSplitEngine {
  /** Feed one dispatch (forming revision, close, or the next bar) and read the current bar. */
  onBar(bar: BarFrame, ticks: IndicatorTicks | undefined): SizeSplitReading;
  readonly tail: SizeSplitRecord | null;
  readonly sizeRing: SizeRing;
  readonly markerRing: readonly MarkerRingEntry[];
  readonly base: { readonly large: number; readonly small: number };
  readonly anchorStart: number | null;
